<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every integer <i>n</i> ≥ 2 and <i>κ</i> ∈ ℝ, we prove that a complete separable metric space is an <i>n</i>-dimensional Alexandrov space of curvature at least <i>κ</i> if and only if, with reference measure $`\mathcal H^n`$, it is a full-support $`\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\kappa,n)`$ space whose distributional sectional curvature is at least <i>κ</i> in Gigli's original global test classes. This resolves Gigli's characterization conjecture in dimensions at least two.

- **claim-alexandrov-tensor-equivalence** (classification, [source](#stmt-main-equivalence)): Let $n\ge2$ be an integer, let $\kappa\in\mathbb R$, and let $(M,d)$ be a complete separable metric space. The following conditions are equivalent.* 1. *$(M,d)$ is an $n$-dimensional Alexandrov space with curvature bounded below by $\kappa$.* 2. *With $m=\mathcal H^n_d$, the space $(M,d,m)$ has full support, is $\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\kappa,n)$, and satisfies $$\label{eq:main-curvature} R(X,Y,Y,X)(f)\ge \kappa\int_M f\bigl(\|X\|^2\|Y\|^2-\langle X,Y\rangle^2\bigr)\,\mathrm dm$$ for every $X,Y\in\operatorname{TestV}(M)$ and every nonnegative $f\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$.* *The $\mathrm{RCD}$ condition is unreduced, and the test classes and curvature sign are exactly those defined above.
  - Explanation: Characterizes Alexandrov lower curvature bounds by the unreduced synthetic Ricci condition, exact Hausdorff measure, full support, and the distributional sectional bound on the original global test classes.
- **claim-cutoffs-and-test-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-pre-approximation)): The following approximations are available.* 1. *For a compact set $E$ in an open set $U$, there is $\chi\in\operatorname{Test}^\infty$ with $0\le\chi\le1$, equal to one on a neighborhood of $E$, and compactly supported in $U$. There are also exhausting cutoffs $\chi_R\in\operatorname{Test}^\infty$, equal to one on $B_R(o)$, supported in $B_{R+1}(o)$, whose gradient and Laplacian bounds are independent of $R$.* 2. *For $g\in D(\Delta)$, $$\label{eq:pre-hessian-estimate} \\|H_g\\|_2^2\le \\|\Delta g\\|_2^2+\max\{-K,0\}\\|\nabla g\\|_2^2.$$ Compactly supported functions in $\operatorname{Test}^\infty$ form a core for $\Delta$.* 3. *On a fixed compact region, a function $g\in\operatorname{Test}$ can be approximated by compactly supported $g_j\in\operatorname{Test}^\infty$ with uniformly bounded gradients, with $$g_j\to g,\qquad \nabla g_j\to\nabla g,\qquad \Delta g_j\to\Delta g,\qquad H_{g_j}\to H_g \quad\hbox{strongly in }L^2$$ on that region.* 4. *A compactly supported Lipschitz function $\xi$ can be approximated by compactly supported test functions $\xi_j$ with a common compact support and a common Lipschitz bound, uniformly and strongly in $W^{1,2}$. If $\xi\ge0$, the approximants may be chosen nonnegative.
  - Explanation: Supplies local and exhausting cutoffs, a compact generator core, strong local second-order approximation, and approximation of Lipschitz weights preserving nonnegativity.
- **claim-tensor-continuity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pre-tensor-continuity)): For fixed test fields the formula <a href="#eq:curvature-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curvature-definition">[eq:curvature-definition]</a> defines a functional on compactly supported Lipschitz weights. It has the form $$f\longmapsto\int fA+\langle\nabla f,B\rangle\,\mathrm dm, \qquad A\in L^1_{\rm loc}(m),\quad B\in L^2_{\rm loc}(TM).$$ It is continuous under the weight approximation in Lemma <a href="#pre:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:approximation">2</a><span class="upright">(iv)</span>. More generally, for weights with a common compact support, uniform convergence of the weights and weak $L^2$ convergence of their gradients suffice. The tensor identities remain valid for these weights. Moreover, on a fixed compact set the functional is continuous under uniformly bounded field approximations for which the fields, their divergences and their covariant derivatives converge strongly in $L^2$, with the fields converging almost everywhere along a subsequence.
  - Explanation: Extends the curvature functional to compact Lipschitz weights and states the weight and field convergence topologies that preserve its evaluation.
- **claim-alexandrov-implies-global-tensor-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fwd-curvature)): Let $(M,d)$ be a complete $n$-dimensional Alexandrov space with curvature bounded below by $\kappa$, where $n\geq2$, and put $m=\mathcal H^n_d$. Then $(M,d,m)$ is $\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\kappa,n)$, its measure has full support, and $$\label{fwd:target} R(X,Y,Y,X)(f) \geq \kappa\int f\bigl(\|X\|^2\|Y\|^2-\langle X,Y\rangle^2\bigr)\,\mathrm dm$$ for every nonnegative $f\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$ and every $X,Y\in\operatorname{TestV}(M)$, with the global test classes and curvature convention of Theorem <a href="#main:equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:equivalence">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Proves the forward implication with full support and the original global test quantifiers, including spaces with boundary and without finite total mass assumptions.
- **claim-short-flow-expansion** (construction, [source](#stmt-fwd-flow)): The flows above exist and are invertible modulo null sets. Their forward and inverse compression constants are $1+O(h)$, their displacements are $O(h)$, and their transported densities have support in a common compact set. Uniformly in $r\in[0,1]$, $$\label{fwd:flow-density-bound} \\|\rho^h_{r,A}-f\\|_{L^\infty}=O(h).$$ Moreover, $$\label{fwd:flow-first} \frac{\rho^h_{r,A}-f}{h}\rightharpoonup^* rL_A$$ in $L^\infty$ on the common bounded support, both at each fixed $r$ and on $[0,1]\times M$. For every fixed $q\in W^{1,2}(M)$, $$\label{fwd:flow-second} \int q\rho^h_A =\int qf+h\int qL_A +\frac{h^2}{2}\int \langle\nabla q,fS_A+L_AA\rangle+o(h^2).$$ If $g$ is Lipschitz on a neighborhood of the common support, then $$\label{fwd:increment} \frac{g(F^h_{1,A}(z))-g(z)}{h} \longrightarrow dg(A)(z) \quad\text{in }L^1(fm).$$
  - Explanation: Constructs invertible Sobolev flows and identifies the transported density variation and endpoint expansions while the correction fields remain fixed.
- **claim-fourth-order-configuration-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-fwd-configuration)): For the above centered finite law and flows, put $$a^h_A(z):=d(z,F^h_{1,A}(z)),\qquad e^h_{AB}(z):=d(F^h_{1,A}(z),F^h_{1,B}(z)).$$ Then $$\label{fwd:distance-first} \frac{a^h_A}{h}\longrightarrow \|A\|,\qquad \frac{e^h_{AB}}h\longrightarrow \|A-B\|$$ in measure and in every finite $L^p(fm)$, for each choice of $A,B$. Furthermore, $$\label{fwd:energy-comparison} \liminf_{h\downarrow0} \frac{2\mathbb E J^h_{0A}-\mathbb E J^h_{AB}}{h^4} \geq\frac\kappa6\int f\,\mathbb E\|A\wedge B\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Uses Alexandrov comparison to bound the averaged radial action minus pair transport energy and identifies the leading radial and pair distances.
- **claim-optimal-density-first-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-fwd-density)): Put $D=B-A$ and $C_s=(1-s)A+sB$. The densities $\sigma^h_s$ have common compact support and a uniform $L^\infty$ bound. They satisfy $$\label{fwd:density-bound} \sup_{0\leq s\leq1}\\|\sigma^h_s-f\\|_{L^2}^2=O(h^2), \qquad \frac{\sigma^h_s-f}{h}\rightharpoonup L_{C_s} \quad\text{in }L^2(\,\mathrm ds\,\mathrm dm).$$ Their velocities satisfy $$\label{fwd:velocity-first} \int_0^1\int\sigma^h_s\|v^h_s/h-D\|^2\,\mathrm ds\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Controls optimal interpolation densities and identifies their weak first variation together with strong density-weighted convergence of rescaled velocities.
- **claim-dual-expansion-and-radial-defect** (bound, [source](#stmt-fwd-dual)): With the preceding notation, $$\begin{aligned} J^h_{AB}\geq{}& h\int(u_B-u_A)(\rho^h_B-\rho^h_A) -\frac{h^2}{2}\int f\|D\|^2 -h^3\int_0^1\int f\langle C_s,T_D\rangle\,\mathrm ds \nonumber\\ &\quad+h^4K_{AB}(S)+o(h^4), \label{fwd:dual-expansion}\\ K_{AB}(S):={}& \frac12\int\left[ \langle Q_1,fS_B+L_BB\rangle -\langle Q_0,fS_A+L_AA\rangle\right] \nonumber\\ &\quad-\int_0^1\int \left[L_{C_s}\langle D,Q_s\rangle+\frac f2\|Q_s\|^2\right]\,\mathrm ds. \label{fwd:dual-coefficient} \end{aligned}$$ For the prescribed radial action $J^h_{0A}$, take pair indices $0,A$, zero initial potential and fields, $\rho^h_0=f$, and $q_{0A}=0$. Then <a href="#fwd:dual-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fwd:dual-expansion">[fwd:dual-expansion]</a> holds with equality and fourth-order coefficient $$\label{fwd:radial-defect} K_{0A}(S)+\frac16\int f\|S_A+\nabla_AA\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Derives the pair transport lower expansion from Sobolev dual tests and gives the additional nonnegative defect in the prescribed radial action.
- **claim-square-completion-and-curvature-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fwd-algebra)): For every ordered pair, $$\label{fwd:square-completion} K_{AB}=\frac12\int_0^1 \left[P(C_s,C_s;D,D)-\int f\|Q_s+\nabla_{C_s}D\|^2\right]\,\mathrm ds.$$ For the radial coefficients use $q_{0A}=0$. Then $$\begin{aligned} 2\mathbb E K_{0A}-\mathbb E K_{AB} ={}&\frac13\mathbb E [P(A,B;A,B)-P(A,A;B,B)] \nonumber\\ &\quad+\frac12\mathbb E\int f\|\nabla q_{AB}+\nabla_A(B-A)\|^2 \label{fwd:average-P}\\ ={}&\frac16\mathbb ER(A,B,B,A)(f) +\frac12\mathbb E\int f\|\nabla q_{AB}+\nabla_A(B-A)\|^2. \label{fwd:average-curvature} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Completes the coefficient square and identifies its centered average with the specified curvature contraction plus a nonnegative gradient discrepancy.
- **claim-localized-curvature-defect** (bound, [source](#stmt-fwd-defect)): For the finite centered law above, every semiconcave compactly supported Lipschitz $f\geq0$, and every family $q_{AB}\in W^{1,2}(M)$, $$\label{fwd:defective-curvature} \frac\kappa6\int f\,\mathbb E\|A\wedge B\|^2 \leq\frac16\mathbb ER(A,B,B,A)(f) +\frac12\mathbb E\int f\|\nabla q_{AB}+\nabla_A(B-A)\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Combines geometric and dual bounds after removing endpoint acceleration defects, leaving a weighted gradient approximation error.
- **claim-semiconcave-weight-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-fwd-weight-approximation)): If $f\geq0$ is compactly supported and Lipschitz, then there are nonnegative compactly supported semiconcave functions $f_j$, with support in $\operatorname{supp}f$, such that $f_j\to f$ uniformly and weakly in $W^{1,2}$, and $\sup_j\operatorname{Lip}(f_j)<\infty$. Consequently <a href="#fwd:defective-curvature" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fwd:defective-curvature">[fwd:defective-curvature]</a> holds for every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz weight.
  - Explanation: Approximates a nonnegative Lipschitz weight by supported semiconcave weights and extends the defective inequality using tensor continuity.
- **claim-finite-gradient-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-fwd-gradient-selection)): Let $f\geq0$ be compactly supported and Lipschitz, and let $V_1,\ldots,V_N\in L^2(TM)$. For every $\eta>0$, there are finitely many nonnegative Lipschitz functions $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_L$, with $\sum_{\ell=1}^L\alpha_\ell=1$, and functions $q_{r,\ell}\in W^{1,2}(M)$, such that $$\label{fwd:selection-error} \sum_{r=1}^N\sum_{\ell=1}^L \int f\alpha_\ell\|\nabla q_{r,\ell}-V_r\|^2<\eta.$$ The products $f\alpha_\ell$ are compactly supported Lipschitz functions. No bound for $\nabla\alpha_\ell$ independent of $\eta$ is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: Approximates a finite list of Sobolev-module fields by gradients selected on Lipschitz localization pieces with arbitrarily small weighted error.
- **claim-compact-gradient-curvature-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-fwd-gradient-bound)): For every $U,V\in\mathcal G$ and every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz $f$, $$\label{fwd:core-bound} R(U,V,V,U)(f)\geq\kappa\int f\|U\wedge V\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Removes the localized square error and recovers the sectional lower bound for compact bounded-Laplacian test gradients.
- **claim-variable-test-coefficients** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fwd-coefficients)): Fix $E_1,\ldots,E_N\in\mathcal G$. Then <a href="#fwd:target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fwd:target">[fwd:target]</a> holds for nonnegative compact test weights and fields of the form $$X=\sum_{i=1}^N a_iE_i, \qquad Y=\sum_{i=1}^N b_iE_i, \qquad a_i,b_i\in\operatorname{Test}(M).$$
  - Explanation: Extends the gradient bound to variable test coefficients through positivity and signed Radon representations of grouped polynomial coefficients.
- **claim-local-strong-field-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-fwd-field-approximation)): Let $b\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$, and let $E\subset M$ be compact. There are compactly supported test functions $b_j$ with bounded Laplacian such that their gradients are uniformly bounded and, on a fixed neighborhood of $E$, $$b_j\to b,\quad\nabla b_j\to\nabla b,\quad \Delta b_j\to\Delta b,\quad \operatorname{Hess}b_j\to\operatorname{Hess}b$$ strongly in $L^2$. The gradients converge almost everywhere after passage to a subsequence. Consequently, for any finite collection of test vector fields, there are fields built with test coefficients and potentials of this compact bounded-Laplacian class which converge locally to the given fields, uniformly bounded in norm, with strong $L^2$ convergence of the fields, their covariant derivatives, and their divergences.
  - Explanation: Approximates test potentials locally with bounded gradients and strong convergence of Hessians and Laplacians, yielding the corresponding field, divergence, and covariant derivative convergence.
- **claim-calibrated-path-index-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heat-index)): Let $n\ge2$ be an integer and let $(M,d,m)$, with $m=\mathcal H^n$, be a full-support $\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\kappa,n)$ space satisfying <a href="#eq:main-curvature" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-curvature">[eq:main-curvature]</a> for every global $X,Y\in\operatorname{TestV}(M)$ and every nonnegative $f\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$. Let $u$ be bounded globally Lipschitz and constant outside a compact set, and let $\Phi_t=Q_tu$. There is a Borel prescription $S:x\mapsto S(x)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Geo}}(M)$, defined outside one $m$-null set, with $S(x)_1=x$ and $S(x)$ satisfying <a href="#heat:calibration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="heat:calibration">[heat:calibration]</a>. For every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz $f$, write $\pi_f=S_\#(fm)$, with forward velocity $v$. The plan has terminal measure $fm$, uniformly bounded speed, and bounded compression on every $[\delta,1]$, $\delta>0$.* *Let $Z$ be a compactly supported test gradient. For every $0<\delta<1$ and every trial <a href="#heat:trial-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="heat:trial-class">[heat:trial-class]</a> with the global endpoint identities $Y_\delta=0$ and $Y_1=Z$, one has $$\label{heat:index-inequality} H_{\Phi_1}(Z,Z)(f) \le\int_{\mathop{\mathrm{Geo}}(M)}\int_\delta^1 \left(\|\partial_tY+\nabla_vY\|^2 -\kappa\|Y\wedge v\|^2\right)(\gamma_t) \,\mathrm dt\,\mathrm d\pi_f(\gamma).$$ The left side is the distributional evaluation <a href="#eq:weak-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-hessian">[eq:weak-hessian]</a>; the right side is finite. Neither the path prescription nor its velocity depends on the choice of $Z$, the trial, or the terminal density.
  - Explanation: Bounds the weak Hessian of a Hopf–Lax potential by trial energies on a common calibrated path prescription independent of the terminal density and trial.
- **claim-heat-estimates-and-bounded-data** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-analytic-inputs)): Let $P_s=\exp(s\Delta)$. On $0<s\le S<\infty$ one has $$\begin{aligned} \label{heat:bakry-one} \|\nabla P_sh\|&\le e^{-Ks}P_s\|\nabla h\|,\\ \label{heat:reverse-poincare} \|\nabla P_sh\|^2&\le C_Ss^{-1}P_s(h^2),\\ \label{heat:laplacian-estimate} \|\Delta P_sh\|^2&\le C_Ss^{-1}P_s(\|\nabla h\|^2), \end{aligned}$$ with the usual Sobolev hypothesis for the first and third inequalities and $h\in L^2$ for the second. In particular, $$\label{heat:laplacian-from-values} \|\Delta P_sh\|^2\le C_Ss^{-2}P_s(h^2).$$ Positive-time heat regularization of an $L^2\cap L^\infty$ function has bounded Lipschitz representatives for all its generator powers. These assertions extend to bounded data, by local approximation, whenever the right-hand sides are defined by the heat kernel.* *For every bounded nonnegative, nonzero datum $h$, and $0<s\le1$, the locally defined logarithmic Laplacian satisfies $$\label{heat:li-yau-consequence} \Delta\log P_sh\ge -C\left(s^{-1}+s\|\nabla\log P_sh\|^2\right).$$ All logarithmic statements are local on regions where the positive-time solution has a positive lower bound.
  - Explanation: Provides gradient and Laplacian regularization estimates, positive-time generator regularity, and a local logarithmic Laplacian lower bound for bounded data.
- **claim-heat-factor-regularity-and-traces** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-regularity)): For each fixed $c>0$, on compact spatial sets and compact time intervals in $(0,1)$, the factors in <a href="#heat:two-factors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="heat:two-factors">[heat:two-factors]</a> have bounded Lipschitz generator powers and positive lower bounds. Their logarithms are local test functions, with locally bounded Sobolev Laplacians. The density is a positive local test function. Time differentiation of the potentials is valid locally in the Laplacian graph norm, hence also for their $L^2$ Hessians.* *There is a constant $L$, independent of $c$ and $t$, such that $$\label{heat:uniform-gradients} \|\nabla\phi\|+\|\nabla\psi\|+\|v_c\|+\|w_c\|\le L.$$ The following identities hold on $(0,1)$: $$\begin{aligned} \label{heat:continuity} \partial_t\rho&=-\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\rho v_c),& \nabla\rho&=\rho w_c/c,\\ \label{heat:hamilton-jacobi} \partial_t\theta&=-\tfrac12(\|v_c\|^2+\|w_c\|^2)-c\Delta b,& \partial_t\phi&=-\tfrac12\|\nabla\phi\|^2+c\Delta\phi. \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\int\rho_t\,\mathrm dm=1$, $\phi_t$ is uniformly bounded, and, for fixed $c$, as $t\uparrow1$, $$\label{heat:terminal-traces} \rho_t\longrightarrow f,\qquad \theta_t\longrightarrow\phi_1+c\log f \quad\text{locally uniformly and strongly in }W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}.$$ The local Lipschitz bounds for $\rho_t$ near the endpoint may depend on the fixed $c$. The bound for $\nabla\theta_t$ is uniform for $0<c\le1$, and the terminal density is always the fixed function $f$.
  - Explanation: Establishes local heat-factor regularity, uniform velocity bounds, the continuity and Hamilton–Jacobi equations, and fixed-viscosity terminal traces.
- **claim-terminal-kernel-ratio** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-kernel-ratio)): For each $A<\infty$ there is $C_A<\infty$ such that, if $0<s\le1$, $g>0$ is bounded and $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}(\log g)\sqrt{s}\le A$, then $$\label{heat:kernel-ratio-estimate} \frac{(P_sg^2)^{1/2}}{P_sg}\le C_A \qquad\text{everywhere.}$$ The constant is independent of the base point and of a positive lower bound for $g$.
  - Explanation: Controls the heat moment ratio uniformly in the base point without requiring a global positive lower bound for the datum.
- **claim-density-and-one-sided-laplacian-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-density-laplacian)): For each $0<\delta<1$ there are $C_\delta$ and nonnegative functions $e_c$ on $(\delta,1)$ such that $$\label{heat:density-and-error} \sup_{\delta\le t\le1}\\|\rho_t^c\\|_\infty\le C_\delta, \qquad c\Delta b^c\ge-e_c(t), \qquad \\|e_c\\|_{L^1(\delta,1)}\longrightarrow0.$$ The lower bound holds also for quasi-continuous representatives, for almost every time.
  - Explanation: Combines uniform positive-time density bounds with a vanishing integrable lower error for the viscosity-scaled Laplacian, including quasi-continuous representatives.
- **claim-weighted-second-order-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-weighted-estimates)): For every bounded set $E$ and $0<\delta<1$, $$\label{heat:weighted-estimate} \int_\delta^1\!\int_E\rho^c \left(\|H_{b^c}\|^2+\|H_{\theta^c}\|^2 +\|\Delta b^c\|^2+\|\Delta\theta^c\|^2 +\frac{\|w_c\|^4}{c^2}\right)\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt \le C_{\delta,E}.$$ Furthermore, for every fixed $T\in L^2((\delta,1)\times E)$, $$\label{heat:weighted-small-w} \int_\delta^1\!\int_E\rho^c\|w_c\|^2\|T\|^2\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Controls weighted Hessian, Laplacian, and density-gradient energies up to the terminal time, and proves vanishing products against fixed square-integrable coefficients.
- **claim-weak-riccati-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heat-riccati-identity)): Let $z$ be a local test function, $a=\nabla z$, $Y$ a compact test gradient, and $h$ a compact Lipschitz multiplier. Define $$\label{heat:covariant-hessian-distribution} (\nabla_aH_z)(Y,Y)(h) =-\int H_z(Y,Y)\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(ha)\,\mathrm dm -2\int hH_z(\nabla_aY,Y)\,\mathrm dm,$$ whenever these products are integrable. Then $$\label{heat:riccati-algebra} H_{\|a\|^2/2}(Y,Y)(h) =(\nabla_aH_z)(Y,Y)(h) +\int h\|H_zY\|^2\,\mathrm dm+R(Y,a,a,Y)(h).$$ For the heat potentials and density of Lemma <a href="#heat:regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="heat:regularity">20</a>, every admissible trial satisfies on $(\delta,1)$ $$\begin{aligned} \label{heat:riccati-evolution} \frac d{dt}\int\rho H_\theta(Y,Y)\,\mathrm dm ={}&\int\rho\left[ 2H_\theta(\partial_tY+\nabla_{v_c}Y,Y)-\|H_\theta Y\|^2 \right]\,\mathrm dm\notag\\ &-R(Y,v_c,v_c,Y)(\rho) -H_{\|w_c\|^2/2+c\Delta b}(Y,Y)(\rho). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Derives the differentiated-Hessian identity using weak first-order calculus and obtains the weighted Riccati evolution without assuming a third covariant derivative.
- **claim-uniform-acceleration-error-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-error-cancellation)): For every admissible trial $Y$, there are numbers $\varepsilon_c\to0$ such that, for every $\delta<T<1$, $$\label{heat:error-lower-bound} \int_\delta^T H_{\|w_c\|^2/2+c\Delta b^c}(Y_t,Y_t)(\rho_t^c)\,\mathrm dt \ge-\varepsilon_c.$$ The bound is independent of the terminal exhaustion parameter $T$.
  - Explanation: Cancels inverse-viscosity terms before estimating Sobolev remainders and singular Bochner measures, uniformly in terminal exhaustion.
- **claim-viscous-index-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-heat-viscous-index)): If the trial <a href="#heat:trial-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="heat:trial-class">[heat:trial-class]</a> satisfies $Y_\delta=0$, then $$\begin{aligned} \label{heat:viscous-index-estimate} H_{\phi_1^c+c\log f}(Y_1,Y_1)(f) \le\int_\delta^1\!\int\rho_t^c \left(\|\partial_tY+\nabla_{v_c}Y\|^2 -\kappa\|Y\wedge v_c\|^2\right)\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt+\varepsilon_c, \end{aligned}$$ where $\varepsilon_c\to0$.
  - Explanation: Integrates the Riccati identity, bounds the acceleration error, and takes the terminal trace while viscosity remains fixed.
- **claim-logarithmic-heat-limit** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-heat-laplace-principle)): As $c\downarrow0$, $$\label{heat:potential-limit} \phi_t^c\longrightarrow\Phi_t=Q_tu \quad\text{locally uniformly on }(0,1]\times M.$$ At each fixed positive time the convergence is also weak in $W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the logarithmic heat limit with the Hopf–Lax potential locally uniformly and weakly in the local Sobolev topology.
- **claim-density-curve-compactness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-heat-curve-compactness)): For each $0<\delta<1$, the probability curves $\mu_t^c=\rho_t^c m$ are uniformly Lipschitz in $W_2$ on $[\delta,1]$, with a constant independent of $c$. Their second moments are uniformly integrable. Consequently, from any sequence $c\downarrow0$ one can extract a subsequence such that $\mu_t^c$ converges uniformly in $W_2$ on every compact positive-time interval, including time one, to a curve $\mu_t=\nu_t m$. Moreover, $0\le\nu_t\le C_\delta$ on $[\delta,1]$.
  - Explanation: Proves uniform Wasserstein continuity and moment-tail control before extracting density-curve limits with bounded compression.
- **claim-action-equality-and-strong-velocity-limit** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-heat-action-limit)): Along a subsequence as in Lemma <a href="#heat:curve-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="heat:curve-compactness">28</a>, there is a measurable vector field $V_t$, with $\|V_t\|\le L$ $\nu_t\,\mathrm dt\,\mathrm dm$-almost everywhere, such that $\partial_t\nu+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\nu V)=0$. For every $0<\alpha<\beta\le1$, $$\begin{aligned} \label{heat:exact-action-identity} \left[\int\phi_t^c\rho_t^c\,\mathrm dm\right]_\alpha^\beta &=\frac12\int_\alpha^\beta\!\int \rho_t^c\|\nabla\phi_t^c\|^2\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt\notag\\ &=\frac12\int_\alpha^\beta\!\int \rho_t^c\|v_c\|^2\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt+o(1). \end{aligned}$$ The limiting curves are constant-speed Wasserstein geodesics on these intervals, and $$\label{heat:strong-weighted-velocity} \int_\alpha^\beta\!\int \rho_t^c\|v_c-V_t\|^2\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt\longrightarrow0.$$ For every fixed $L^2$ tensor coefficient $T$, compactly supported in space-time in $[\alpha,\beta]\times M$, $$\label{heat:strong-coefficient-velocity} \int_\alpha^\beta\!\int \rho_t^c\|T(v_c-V_t)\|^2\,\mathrm dm\,\mathrm dt\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Uses an exact action identity and the Hopf–Lax action sandwich to identify geodesic limits and strengthen flux convergence to weighted velocity convergence.
- **claim-common-calibrated-path-prescription** (construction, [source](#stmt-heat-common-paths)): There is a Borel prescription $S:x\mapsto S(x)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Geo}}(M)$, defined outside one $m$-null set and with $S(x)_1=x$, such that $S(x)$ satisfies <a href="#heat:calibration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="heat:calibration">[heat:calibration]</a>. For every $f\in\mathcal F$, the limiting density curves are the marginals of $\pi_f=S_\#(fm)$. These plans have speed bounded by $\sqrt{2\operatorname{osc}(u)}$ and bounded compression on each $[\delta,1]$. Their forward path velocities agree with the pullback of the vector field $V$ in Lemma <a href="#heat:action-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="heat:action-limit">29</a>; in particular there is no conditional averaging loss. The same prescription defines $\pi_f$ for every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz datum, with the same speed and positive-time compression properties.
  - Explanation: Uses optimal transport uniqueness to obtain a common Borel prescription and identifies path velocities without conditional averaging loss.
- **claim-countable-harmonic-frames** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-countable-frames)): There is a countable family $\{g_j\}_{j\geq1}$ of compact test functions, a set $N$ of $2$-capacity zero, and a Borel set $S\subset\mathcal R_n\setminus N$ with $\mathcal H^{n-1}(S)=0$ such that the following holds. At every $x\in\mathcal R_n\setminus(N\cup S)$, some $n$ members of $\{\nabla g_j\}$ have positive definite Gram matrix, evaluated in common precise Sobolev representatives. All finite Gram matrices are positive semidefinite and have rank at most $n$ outside $N$. Any prescribed countable family of compact test functions may be included among the $g_j$.
  - Explanation: Selects countably many compact test gradients that provide full-rank frames at regular points outside a polar set and a Hausdorff-null rank defect.
- **claim-local-content-implies-hausdorff-nullity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-frames-content-null)): Let $E$ be a subset of a separable metric space and let $s>0$. If $$\label{frames:vanishing-content} \mathcal H^s_\infty(E\cap B(x,r))=o(r^s) \quad\text{as }r\downarrow0\quad\text{for every }x\in E,$$ then $\mathcal H^s(E)=0$. It suffices to assume <a href="#frames:vanishing-content" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="frames:vanishing-content">[frames:vanishing-content]</a> along all sufficiently small dyadic radii.
  - Explanation: Turns pointwise vanishing local Hausdorff content into Hausdorff nullity, including control available at all sufficiently small dyadic radii.
- **claim-rank-defect-content-energy-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-energy-content)): Work in a bounded region where <a href="#eq:local-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-volume">[eq:local-volume]</a> and the Poincaré inequality hold with fixed constants. There are an enlargement factor $L$ and $\varepsilon_0>0$ with the following property. Let $P$ be a bounded Sobolev matrix on $B(x,Lr)$ and suppose $$\int_{B(x,Lr)}\|P-I\|\,\mathrm dm\leq\varepsilon_0r^n.$$ For $n-2<s$ let $E\subset B(x,r)$ consist of precise-representative points of $P$ at which $\|P-I\|\geq1$. Then $$\label{frames:energy-content-bound} \mathcal H^s_\infty(E) \leq C_s r^{s+2-n} \int_{B(x,Lr)}\|\nabla P\|^2\,\mathrm dm.$$ The constants depend only on $s,n$ and the local volume and Poincaré constants.
  - Explanation: Bounds the content of a Sobolev matrix rank defect by local Dirichlet energy under a small mean deviation and a strictly admissible content exponent.
- **claim-entire-path-avoidance** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-frames-path-avoidance)): Let $a<b$ and let $\pi$ be a finite plan concentrated on $L$-Lipschitz paths with bounded positions. Suppose $(e_t)_\#\pi\leq C m$ for every $t\in[a,b]$. For each fixed polar set $N$ and each fixed $\mathcal H^{n-1}$-null set $S$, almost every path satisfies $\gamma([a,b])\cap(N\cup S)=\varnothing$. For any countable family of Sobolev functions, their quasi-continuous representatives agree, along almost every entire path, with the absolutely continuous representatives furnished by Sobolev differentiation along plans.
  - Explanation: Shows entire-path avoidance of fixed polar and Hausdorff-null sets, and matches quasi-continuous Sobolev representatives with continuous path representatives.
- **claim-geodesic-regularity-at-every-time** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-frames-regular-paths)): Let $\pi$ be a bounded-position geodesic plan on $[a,b]$ whose marginals have uniformly bounded densities. Then almost every path lies in $\mathcal R_n$ for every $t\in[a,b]$.
  - Explanation: Combines bounded-density regularity at a dense set of times with tangent-cone continuity to obtain regularity at every time on almost every geodesic.
- **claim-finite-dimensional-path-calculus** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-path-calculus)): Fix a countable family as in Proposition <a href="#frames:countable-frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:countable-frames">31</a>. Let $\pi$ be an optimal geodesic plan on $[a,b]$ with bounded positions, bounded speed, and uniformly bounded-compression marginals. Along almost every path there are $n$-dimensional fibers at every time and local frames formed from the specified test gradients, with the following properties.* 1. *Gram entries belong to $W^{1,2}([a,b])$, are continuous, and differentiate by metric compatibility with $\nabla_v$.* 2. *In a local frame, fields with $W^{1,2}$ scalar coefficients have a well-defined metric-compatible derivative $$\label{frames:curve-derivative} \mathcal D\left(\sum_i a_i e_i\right) =\sum_i\bigl(a_i'e_i+a_i\nabla_v e_i\bigr).$$ Its connection coefficients belong to $L^2$. There are orthonormal parallel frames on the entire interval.* 3. *The geodesic velocity has a continuous representative in these fibers, satisfies $\mathcal Dv=0$, and has norm equal to the constant metric speed. At $a$ and $b$ the fibers and all specified test gradients agree with their tangent-module counterparts almost surely.
  - Explanation: Constructs continuous path fibers, metric-compatible derivatives, parallel orthonormal frames, and measurable endpoint velocity traces from countably many test gradients.
- **claim-countable-pathwise-trial-density** (construction, [source](#stmt-frames-trial-density)): Fix $0<\delta<1$, a compact test gradient $Z$, and a countable family in Proposition <a href="#frames:countable-frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:countable-frames">31</a> whose gradients include $Z$. There is a countable collection $\mathcal Y_{\delta,Z}$ of fields that are smooth temporal linear combinations of these gradients and satisfy $$Y_\delta=0,\qquad Y_1=Z$$ as identities of fields on $M$, with the following property. For every optimal geodesic plan with the bounds of Proposition <a href="#frames:path-calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:path-calculus">36</a> on $[\delta,1]$, along almost every path every $W^{1,2}$ field $W$ satisfying $W(\delta)=0$ and $W(1)=Z(\gamma_1)$ is a limit of members of $\mathcal Y_{\delta,Z}$ in the norm $$\left(\int_\delta^1 (\|Y-W\|^2+\|\mathcal DY-\mathcal DW\|^2)\,\mathrm dt\right)^{1/2}.$$ The approximating sequence may depend on the path and on $W$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a countable family of global endpoint-preserving trials dense separately along almost every path; the approximating sequence may depend on that path.
- **claim-radial-terminal-gradient-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frames-terminal-trace)): For the radial calibrated paths just described, the terminal representative supplied by Proposition <a href="#frames:path-calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:path-calculus">36</a> satisfies $$\label{frames:terminal-identity} v(1)=\nabla q(\gamma_1)$$ almost surely. In particular $\|\nabla q\|=r$ almost everywhere on $B(p,R_\kappa)$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the continuous terminal velocity of calibrated radial paths with the gradient of half squared distance, including zero speed.
- **claim-distributional-radial-hessian-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-frames-hessian)): Let $n\ge2$ be an integer, let $\kappa\in\mathbb R$, and let $(M,d,m)$, with $m=\mathcal H^n$, be a full-support $\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\kappa,n)$ space satisfying <a href="#eq:main-curvature" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-curvature">[eq:main-curvature]</a> for every global $X,Y\in\operatorname{TestV}(M)$ and every nonnegative $f\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$. Fix $R_\kappa>0$, with $R_\kappa\sqrt\kappa<\pi/2$ if $\kappa>0$. For every $p\in M$, put $r=d(p,\cdot)$ and $q=r^2/2$. Then for every compact test gradient $Z$, $\operatorname{Hess}q(Z,Z)$ on $B(p,R_\kappa)$ is a signed Radon measure whose singular part with respect to $m$ is nonpositive. It satisfies $$\label{frames:hessian-bound} \operatorname{Hess}q(Z,Z) \leq\left( \|Z_\parallel\|^2+ r\frac{\mathfrak s_\kappa'(r)}{\mathfrak s_\kappa(r)} \|Z_\perp\|^2\right)m.$$ Here $\|\nabla q\|=r$ almost everywhere, and on $\{r>0\}$ $$Z_\parallel=\frac{\langle Z,\nabla q\rangle}{r^2}\nabla q, \qquad Z_\perp=Z-Z_\parallel.$$ At $r=0$ the coefficient on the right-hand side of <a href="#frames:hessian-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="frames:hessian-bound">[frames:hessian-bound]</a> is interpreted as $\|Z\|^2$. The measure inequality holds, in particular, against every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz weight on the ball. The radius $R_\kappa$ is independent of $p$.
  - Explanation: Obtains a signed Radon Hessian measure with nonpositive singular part and the radial and transverse model comparison bound using a countable infimum of trial energies.
- **claim-weak-hessian-bound-on-every-geodesic** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-weight-every-geodesic)): Let $(M,d,m)$ be a full-support $\mathrm{RCD}(K,N)$ space, where $K\in\mathbb R$ and $1<N<\infty$. Let $F$ be bounded and globally Lipschitz, and let $G$ be bounded and continuous. Suppose that for every compactly supported $g\in\operatorname{Test}(M)$ and every nonnegative $h\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}_c(M)$, $$\label{weight:hessian-assumption} H_F(\nabla g,\nabla g)(h) \le \int hG\|\nabla g\|^2\,\mathrm dm,$$ where the weak Hessian is defined by <a href="#eq:weak-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weak-hessian">[eq:weak-hessian]</a>. Then every constant-speed minimizing geodesic $\sigma:[0,1]\to M$, of length $\ell$, satisfies $$\label{weight:curve-hessian} (F\circ\sigma)''\le\ell^2G\circ\sigma \quad\text{in distributions on }(0,1).$$
  - Explanation: Recalls the companion result transferring a weak Hessian upper bound to every minimizing geodesic in general finite-dimensional full-support RCD spaces.
- **claim-global-bounded-distance-modification** (construction, [source](#stmt-weight-distance-modification)): Assume the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#frames:hessian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="frames:hessian">40</a>, and write $R_*=R_\kappa>0$ for its radius. There are numbers $0<a<b<R_*$, depending only on $\kappa$ and the chosen $R_*$, such that for every $p\in M$ there are bounded globally Lipschitz $F_p$ and bounded continuous $G_p$ satisfying <a href="#weight:hessian-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weight:hessian-assumption">[weight:hessian-assumption]</a> and $$\label{weight:local-modification} F_p=\mathfrak m_\kappa(d(p,\cdot)),\qquad G_p=1-\kappa F_p\quad\text{on }B_a(p).$$ For $\kappa>0$, we may require $b<\pi/(2\sqrt\kappa)$.
  - Explanation: Flattens model distance outside a uniform local ball to meet the global bounded weak-Hessian interface while preserving the model equation near the center.
- **claim-tensor-bound-implies-alexandrov-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weight-alexandrov)): Under hypothesis <span class="upright">(B)</span> of Theorem <a href="#main:equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:equivalence">1</a>, $(M,d)$ is an $n$-dimensional Alexandrov space of curvature at least $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Derives point-on-side comparison from the model differential inequality, obtains local four-point comparison, and globalizes the Alexandrov lower bound with the stated Hausdorff dimension.

        "anchor": "stmt-fwd-field-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b\\in\\operatorname{Test}(M)$, and let $E\\subset M$ be compact. There are compactly supported test functions $b_j$ with bounded Laplacian such that their gradients are uniformly bounded and, on a fixed neighborhood of $E$, $$b_j\\to b,\\quad\\nabla b_j\\to\\nabla b,\\quad\n \\Delta b_j\\to\\Delta b,\\quad \\operatorname{Hess}b_j\\to\\operatorname{Hess}b$$ strongly in $L^2$. The gradients converge almost everywhere after passage to a subsequence. Consequently, for any finite collection of test vector fields, there are fields built with test coefficients and potentials of this compact bounded-Laplacian class which converge locally to the given fields, uniformly bounded in norm, with strong $L^2$ convergence of the fields, their covariant derivatives, and their divergences.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heat-index",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge2$ be an integer and let $(M,d,m)$, with $m=\\mathcal H^n$, be a full-support $\\mathrm{RCD}((n-1)\\kappa,n)$ space satisfying <a href=\"#eq:main-curvature\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:main-curvature\">[eq:main-curvature]</a> for every global $X,Y\\in\\operatorname{TestV}(M)$ and every nonnegative $f\\in\\operatorname{Test}(M)$. Let $u$ be bounded globally Lipschitz and constant outside a compact set, and let $\\Phi_t=Q_tu$. There is a Borel prescription $S:x\\mapsto S(x)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Geo}}(M)$, defined outside one $m$-null set, with $S(x)_1=x$ and $S(x)$ satisfying <a href=\"#heat:calibration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"heat:calibration\">[heat:calibration]</a>. For every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz $f$, write $\\pi_f=S_\\#(fm)$, with forward velocity $v$. The plan has terminal measure $fm$, uniformly bounded speed, and bounded compression on every $[\\delta,1]$, $\\delta>0$.*\n\n\n*Let $Z$ be a compactly supported test gradient. For every $0<\\delta<1$ and every trial <a href=\"#heat:trial-class\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"heat:trial-class\">[heat:trial-class]</a> with the global endpoint identities $Y_\\delta=0$ and $Y_1=Z$, one has $$\\label{heat:index-inequality}\nH_{\\Phi_1}(Z,Z)(f)\n\\le\\int_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Geo}}(M)}\\int_\\delta^1\n\\left(|\\partial_tY+\\nabla_vY|^2\n-\\kappa|Y\\wedge v|^2\\right)(\\gamma_t)\n\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_f(\\gamma).$$ The left side is the distributional evaluation <a href=\"#eq:weak-hessian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weak-hessian\">[eq:weak-hessian]</a>; the right side is finite. Neither the path prescription nor its velocity depends on the choice of $Z$, the trial, or the terminal density.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heat-analytic-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P_s=\\exp(s\\Delta)$. On $0<s\\le S<\\infty$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{heat:bakry-one}\n|\\nabla P_sh|&\\le e^{-Ks}P_s|\\nabla h|,\\\\\n\\label{heat:reverse-poincare}\n|\\nabla P_sh|^2&\\le C_Ss^{-1}P_s(h^2),\\\\\n\\label{heat:laplacian-estimate}\n|\\Delta P_sh|^2&\\le C_Ss^{-1}P_s(|\\nabla h|^2),\n\\end{aligned}$$ with the usual Sobolev hypothesis for the first and third inequalities and $h\\in L^2$ for the second. In particular, $$\\label{heat:laplacian-from-values}\n|\\Delta P_sh|^2\\le C_Ss^{-2}P_s(h^2).$$ Positive-time heat regularization of an $L^2\\cap L^\\infty$ function has bounded Lipschitz representatives for all its generator powers. These assertions extend to bounded data, by local approximation, whenever the right-hand sides are defined by the heat kernel.*\n\n\n*For every bounded nonnegative, nonzero datum $h$, and $0<s\\le1$, the locally defined logarithmic Laplacian satisfies $$\\label{heat:li-yau-consequence}\n\\Delta\\log P_sh\\ge\n-C\\left(s^{-1}+s|\\nabla\\log P_sh|^2\\right).$$ All logarithmic statements are local on regions where the positive-time solution has a positive lower bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-heat-analytic-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P_s=\\exp(s\\Delta)$. In particular, $$\\label{heat:laplacian-from-values}\n|\\Delta P_sh|^2\\le C_Ss^{-2}P_s(h^2).$$ Positive-time heat regularization of an $L^2\\cap L^\\infty$ function has bounded Lipschitz representatives for all its generator powers. These assertions extend to bounded data, by local approximation, whenever the right-hand sides are defined by the heat kernel.*\n\n\n*For every bounded nonnegative, nonzero datum $h$, and $0<s\\le1$, the locally defined logarithmic Laplacian satisfies $$\\label{heat:li-yau-consequence}\n\\Delta\\log P_sh\\ge\n-C\\left(s^{-1}+s|\\nabla\\log P_sh|^2\\right).$$ All logarithmic statements are local on regions where the positive-time solution has a positive lower bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-356.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heat-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $c>0$, on compact spatial sets and compact time intervals in $(0,1)$, the factors in <a href=\"#heat:two-factors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"heat:two-factors\">[heat:two-factors]</a> have bounded Lipschitz generator powers and positive lower bounds. Their logarithms are local test functions, with locally bounded Sobolev Laplacians. The density is a positive local test function. Time differentiation of the potentials is valid locally in the Laplacian graph norm, hence also for their $L^2$ Hessians.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*There is a constant $L$, independent of $c$ and $t$, such that $$\\label{heat:uniform-gradients}\n|\\nabla\\phi|+|\\nabla\\psi|+|v_c|+|w_c|\\le L.$$ The following identities hold on $(0,1)$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{heat:continuity}\n\\partial_t\\rho&=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\rho v_c),&\n\\nabla\\rho&=\\rho w_c/c,\\\\\n\\label{heat:hamilton-jacobi}\n\\partial_t\\theta&=-\\tfrac12(|v_c|^2+|w_c|^2)-c\\Delta b,&\n\\partial_t\\phi&=-\\tfrac12|\\nabla\\phi|^2+c\\Delta\\phi.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\int\\rho_t\\,\\mathrm dm=1$, $\\phi_t$ is uniformly bounded, and, for fixed $c$, as $t\\uparrow1$, $$\\label{heat:terminal-traces}\n\\rho_t\\longrightarrow f,\\qquad\n\\theta_t\\longrightarrow\\phi_1+c\\log f\n\\quad\\text{locally uniformly and strongly in }W^{1,2}_{\\rm loc}.$$ The local Lipschitz bounds for $\\rho_t$ near the endpoint may depend on the fixed $c$. The bound for $\\nabla\\theta_t$ is uniform for $0<c\\le1$, and the terminal density is always the fixed function $f$.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-heat-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed $c>0$, on compact spatial sets and compact time intervals in $(0,1)$, the factors in <a href=\"#heat:two-factors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"heat:two-factors\">[heat:two-factors]</a> have bounded Lipschitz generator powers and positive lower bounds. Their logarithms are local test functions, with locally bounded Sobolev Laplacians. The density is a positive local test function. Time differentiation of the potentials is valid locally in the Laplacian graph norm, hence also for their $L^2$ Hessians.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*There is a constant $L$, independent of $c$ and $t$, such that $$\\label{heat:uniform-gradients}\n|\\nabla\\phi|+|\\nabla\\psi|+|v_c|+|w_c|\\le L.$$ The following identities hold on $(0,1)$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{heat:continuity}\n\\partial_t\\rho&=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\rho v_c),&\n\\nabla\\rho&=\\rho w_c/c,\\\\\n\\label{heat:hamilton-jacobi}\n\\partial_t\\theta&=-\\tfrac12(|v_c|^2+|w_c|^2)-c\\Delta b,&\n\\partial_t\\phi&=-\\tfrac12|\\nabla\\phi|^2+c\\Delta\\phi.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\int\\rho_t\\,\\mathrm dm=1$, $\\phi_t$ is uniformly bounded, and, for fixed $c$, as $t\\uparrow1$, $$\\label{heat:terminal-traces}\n\\rho_t\\longrightarrow f,\\qquad\n\\theta_t\\longrightarrow\\phi_1+c\\log f\n\\quad\\text{locally uniformly and strongly in }W^{1,2}_{\\rm loc}.$$ The local Lipschitz bounds for $\\rho_t$ near the endpoint may depend on the fixed $c$. The bound for $\\nabla\\theta_t$ is uniform for $0<c\\le1$, and the terminal density is always the fixed function $f$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heat-density-laplacian",
        "exact_quote": "For each $0<\\delta<1$ there are $C_\\delta$ and nonnegative functions $e_c$ on $(\\delta,1)$ such that $$\\label{heat:density-and-error}\n\\sup_{\\delta\\le t\\le1}\\|\\rho_t^c\\|_\\infty\\le C_\\delta,\n\\qquad c\\Delta b^c\\ge-e_c(t),\n\\qquad \\|e_c\\|_{L^1(\\delta,1)}\\longrightarrow0.$$ The lower bound holds also for quasi-continuous representatives, for almost every time.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $z$ be a local test function, $a=\\nabla z$, $Y$ a compact test gradient, and $h$ a compact Lipschitz multiplier. Define $$\\label{heat:covariant-hessian-distribution}\n(\\nabla_aH_z)(Y,Y)(h)\n=-\\int H_z(Y,Y)\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(ha)\\,\\mathrm dm\n-2\\int hH_z(\\nabla_aY,Y)\\,\\mathrm dm,$$ whenever these products are integrable. Then $$\\label{heat:riccati-algebra}\nH_{|a|^2/2}(Y,Y)(h)\n=(\\nabla_aH_z)(Y,Y)(h)\n+\\int h|H_zY|^2\\,\\mathrm dm+R(Y,a,a,Y)(h).$$ For the heat potentials and density of Lemma <a href=\"#heat:regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"heat:regularity\">20</a>, every admissible trial satisfies on $(\\delta,1)$ $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{heat:riccati-evolution}\n\\frac d{dt}\\int\\rho H_\\theta(Y,Y)\\,\\mathrm dm\n={}&\\int\\rho\\left[\n2H_\\theta(\\partial_tY+\\nabla_{v_c}Y,Y)-|H_\\theta Y|^2\n\\right]\\,\\mathrm dm\\notag\\\\\n&-R(Y,v_c,v_c,Y)(\\rho)\n-H_{|w_c|^2/2+c\\Delta b}(Y,Y)(\\rho).\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-heat-curve-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "For each $0<\\delta<1$, the probability curves $\\mu_t^c=\\rho_t^c m$ are uniformly Lipschitz in $W_2$ on $[\\delta,1]$, with a constant independent of $c$.         "exact_quote": "Along a subsequence as in Lemma <a href=\"#heat:curve-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"heat:curve-compactness\">28</a>, there is a measurable vector field $V_t$, with $|V_t|\\le L$ $\\nu_t\\,\\mathrm dt\\,\\mathrm dm$-almost everywhere, such that $\\partial_t\\nu+\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\nu V)=0$. For every $0<\\alpha<\\beta\\le1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{heat:exact-action-identity}\n\\left[\\int\\phi_t^c\\rho_t^c\\,\\mathrm dm\\right]_\\alpha^\\beta\n&=\\frac12\\int_\\alpha^\\beta\\!\\int\n\\rho_t^c|\\nabla\\phi_t^c|^2\\,\\mathrm dm\\,\\mathrm dt\\notag\\\\\n&=\\frac12\\int_\\alpha^\\beta\\!\\int\n\\rho_t^c|v_c|^2\\,\\mathrm dm\\,\\mathrm dt+o(1).\n\\end{aligned}$$ The limiting curves are constant-speed Wasserstein geodesics on these intervals, and $$\\label{heat:strong-weighted-velocity}\n\\int_\\alpha^\\beta\\!\\int\n\\rho_t^c|v_c-V_t|^2\\,\\mathrm dm\\,\\mathrm dt\\longrightarrow0.$$ For every fixed $L^2$ tensor coefficient $T$, compactly supported in space-time in $[\\alpha,\\beta]\\times M$, $$\\label{heat:strong-coefficient-velocity}\n\\int_\\alpha^\\beta\\!\\int\n\\rho_t^c|T(v_c-V_t)|^2\\,\\mathrm dm\\,\\mathrm dt\\longrightarrow0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heat-common-paths",
        "exact_quote": "There is a Borel prescription $S:x\\mapsto S(x)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Geo}}(M)$, defined outside one $m$-null set and with $S(x)_1=x$, such that $S(x)$ satisfies <a href=\"#heat:calibration\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"heat:calibration\">[heat:calibration]</a>. For every $f\\in\\mathcal F$, the limiting density curves are the marginals of $\\pi_f=S_\\#(fm)$. These plans have speed bounded by $\\sqrt{2\\operatorname{osc}(u)}$ and bounded compression on each $[\\delta,1]$. Their forward path velocities agree with the pullback of the vector field $V$ in Lemma <a href=\"#heat:action-limit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"heat:action-limit\">29</a>; in particular there is no conditional averaging loss. The same prescription defines $\\pi_f$ for every nonnegative compactly supported Lipschitz datum, with the same speed and positive-time compression properties.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-countable-frames",
        "exact_quote": "There is a countable family $\\{g_j\\}_{j\\geq1}$ of compact test functions, a set $N$ of $2$-capacity zero, and a Borel set $S\\subset\\mathcal R_n\\setminus N$ with $\\mathcal H^{n-1}(S)=0$ such that the following holds. At every $x\\in\\mathcal R_n\\setminus(N\\cup S)$, some $n$ members of $\\{\\nabla g_j\\}$ have positive definite Gram matrix, evaluated in common precise Sobolev representatives. All finite Gram matrices are positive semidefinite and have rank at most $n$ outside $N$. Any prescribed countable family of compact test functions may be included among the $g_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Work in a bounded region where <a href=\"#eq:local-volume\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:local-volume\">[eq:local-volume]</a> and the Poincaré inequality hold with fixed constants. Let $P$ be a bounded Sobolev matrix on $B(x,Lr)$ and suppose $$\\int_{B(x,Lr)}|P-I|\\,\\mathrm dm\\leq\\varepsilon_0r^n.$$ For $n-2<s$ let $E\\subset B(x,r)$ consist of precise-representative points of $P$ at which $|P-I|\\geq1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-path-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a countable family as in Proposition <a href=\"#frames:countable-frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:countable-frames\">31</a>. Let $\\pi$ be an optimal geodesic plan on $[a,b]$ with bounded positions, bounded speed, and uniformly bounded-compression marginals. Along almost every path there are $n$-dimensional fibers at every time and local frames formed from the specified test gradients, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Gram entries belong to $W^{1,2}([a,b])$, are continuous, and differentiate by metric compatibility with $\\nabla_v$.*\n\n\n2.  *In a local frame, fields with $W^{1,2}$ scalar coefficients have a well-defined metric-compatible derivative $$\\label{frames:curve-derivative}\n       \\mathcal D\\left(\\sum_i a_i e_i\\right)\n            =\\sum_i\\bigl(a_i'e_i+a_i\\nabla_v e_i\\bigr).$$ Its connection coefficients belong to $L^2$. There are orthonormal parallel frames on the entire interval.*\n\n3.  *The geodesic velocity has a continuous representative in these fibers, satisfies $\\mathcal Dv=0$, and has norm equal to the constant metric speed. At $a$ and $b$ the fibers and all specified test gradients agree with their tangent-module counterparts almost surely.",
        "source_id": "PM-356.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-frames-path-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a countable family as in Proposition <a href=\"#frames:countable-frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:countable-frames\">31</a>. Along almost every path there are $n$-dimensional fibers at every time and local frames formed from the specified test gradients, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Gram entries belong to $W^{1,2}([a,b])$, are continuous, and differentiate by metric compatibility with $\\nabla_v$.*\n\n\n2.  *In a local frame, fields with $W^{1,2}$ scalar coefficients have a well-defined metric-compatible derivative $$\\label{frames:curve-derivative}\n       \\mathcal D\\left(\\sum_i a_i e_i\\right)\n            =\\sum_i\\bigl(a_i'e_i+a_i\\nabla_v e_i\\bigr).$$ Its connection coefficients belong to $L^2$. There are orthonormal parallel frames on the entire interval.*\n\n3.  *The geodesic velocity has a continuous representative in these fibers, satisfies $\\mathcal Dv=0$, and has norm equal to the constant metric speed. At $a$ and $b$ the fibers and all specified test gradients agree with their tangent-module counterparts almost surely.",
          "source_id": "PM-356.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a countable family as in Proposition <a href=\"#frames:countable-frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:countable-frames\">31</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frames-trial-density",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<1$, a compact test gradient $Z$, and a countable family in Proposition <a href=\"#frames:countable-frames\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"frames:countable-frames\">31</a> whose gradients include $Z$. There is a countable collection $\\mathcal Y_{\\delta,Z}$ of fields that are smooth temporal linear combinations of these gradients and satisfy $$Y_\\delta=0,\\qquad Y_1=Z$$ as identities of fields on $M$, with the following property. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Global test functions and test vector fields** ([source](#eq:test-class)): Our test classes are $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:test-class} \operatorname{Test}(M) &=\left\{f\in D(\Delta): \begin{array}{l}f\text{ has a bounded globally Lipschitz representative},\\ \Delta f\in W^{1,2}(M) \end{array}\right\},\\ \label{eq:test-vector-class} \operatorname{TestV}(M) &=\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}a_j\nabla b_j: \ell<\infty,\ a_j,b_j\in\operatorname{Test}(M)\right\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **Nonpositive Laplacian** ([source](#eq:curvature-definition)): On a full-support finite-dimensional $\mathrm{RCD}$ space $(M,d,m)$, we use the nonpositive Laplacian: $$\int_M\langle\nabla f,\nabla h\rangle\,\mathrm dm =-\int_M(\Delta f)h\,\mathrm dm.$$
- **Weak Lie bracket** ([source](#eq:curvature-definition)): For test fields set $[X,Y]=\nabla_XY-\nabla_YX$, where $\nabla$ is the weak Levi–Civita covariant derivative.
- **Distributional curvature functional** ([source](#eq:curvature-definition)): The curvature functional is $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:curvature-definition} R(X,Y,Z,W)(f)=\int_M& -\langle\nabla_X(fW),\nabla_YZ\rangle -f\langle\nabla_YZ,W\rangle\mathop{\mathrm{div}}X \notag\\ &+\langle\nabla_Y(fW),\nabla_XZ\rangle +f\langle\nabla_XZ,W\rangle\mathop{\mathrm{div}}Y \notag\\ &-f\langle\nabla_{[X,Y]}Z,W\rangle\,\mathrm dm. \end{aligned}$$
- **Test functions with bounded Laplacian** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Besides $\operatorname{Test}$, defined in <a href="#eq:test-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:test-class">[eq:test-class]</a>, it is useful to write $$\operatorname{Test}^\infty(M)=\{g\in\operatorname{Test}(M):\Delta g\in L^\infty(M)\}.$$
- **Local test function** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A function is a local test function on an open set if it agrees on every relatively compact smaller open set with a global test function.
- **Weak Hessian evaluation** ([source](#eq:weak-hessian)): For $F\in W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M)$, a compact test gradient $Y=\nabla g$, and a compactly supported Lipschitz weight $\xi$, define $$\label{eq:weak-hessian} H_F(Y,Y)(\xi) =-\int_M\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\xi Y)\,\langle\nabla F,Y\rangle\,\mathrm dm -\int_M\xi\,\langle\nabla F,\nabla_YY\rangle\,\mathrm dm.$$
- **Polar sets and quasi-everywhere statements** ([source](#eq:bochner-dimension)): A polar set has zero $2$-capacity; *quasi-everywhere* means outside a polar set.
- **Wedge magnitude and compact gradient class** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Write $$\|U\wedge V\|^2:=\|U\|^2\|V\|^2-\langle U,V\rangle^2.$$ $$\mathcal G:=\{\nabla u: u\in\operatorname{Test}(M),\ \operatorname{supp}u\text{ compact},\ \Delta u\in L^\infty(M)\}.$$
- **Finite centered gradient law** ([source](#fwd:centering)): We choose a finite probability law on compact test potentials with bounded Laplacian, invariant under $u\mapsto-u$.
- **Transport energy for equal-mass finite measures** ([source](#pm-h-15)): If $\mu,\nu$ are finite nonnegative measures of equal mass, define their transport energy by $$J(\mu,\nu):=\frac12 \inf_{\gamma\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\int d(x,y)^2\,\mathrm d\gamma(x,y).$$
- **Admissible temporal trial** ([source](#heat:trial-class)): For $0<\delta<1$, an admissible trial is a field $$\label{heat:trial-class} Y_t=\sum_{j=1}^q a_j(t)\nabla g_j,\qquad a_j\in C^\infty([\delta,1]),\quad g_j\in\operatorname{Test}(M)\text{ compactly supported}.$$
- **Calibrated geodesic** ([source](#heat:calibration)): A geodesic $\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Geo}}(M)$ is calibrated by $u$ if $$\label{heat:calibration} \Phi_1(\gamma_1) =u(\gamma_0)+\tfrac12d^2(\gamma_0,\gamma_1).$$
- **Regular set** ([source](#sec:frames)): Write $\mathcal R_n$ for the set of points all of whose measured tangents are the normalized Euclidean $n$-space.
- **Modified model distance** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Define the modified model distance by $$\mathfrak m_\kappa(r)=\int_0^r\mathfrak s_\kappa(s)\,\mathrm ds =\begin{cases} (1-\cos(\sqrt\kappa r))/\kappa,&\kappa>0,\\ r^2/2,&\kappa=0,\\ (\cosh(\sqrt{-\kappa}r)-1)/(-\kappa),&\kappa<0. \end{cases}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-dimension-threshold | Dimension parameter | ≥ | $2$ | Integer dimension. |
| q-synthetic-ricci-parameter | Synthetic Ricci lower bound parameter | = | $(n-1)\kappa$ | The dimension and sectional lower bound of the characterization. |
| q-generator-hessian-bound | Squared Hessian norm | ≤ | $\\|\Delta g\\|_2^2+\max\{-K,0\}\\|\nabla g\\|_2^2$ | Generator-domain functions and the ambient Ricci lower bound. |
| q-short-flow-density-rate | Uniform transported-density deviation | = | $O(h)$ | The flow scale tends to zero with correction fields and weight fixed. |
| q-optimal-density-squared-error-rate | Squared optimal interpolation density error | = | $O(h^2)$ | Uniform over interpolation time while the weight and finite field family remain fixed. |
| q-inf-convolution-uniform-error | Uniform inf-convolution error | ≤ | $\frac{\varepsilon L^2}{2}$ | The inf-convolution scale and the fixed weight Lipschitz constant. |
| q-localized-gradient-error | Sum of weighted gradient selection errors | < | $\eta$ | A fixed finite list of square-integrable fields and arbitrary positive tolerance. |
| q-uniform-heat-velocity-bound | Sum of potential-gradient and velocity magnitudes | ≤ | $L$ | Uniform in viscosity and time for the fixed potential and terminal datum. |
| q-terminal-kernel-ratio-bound | Heat moment ratio | ≤ | $C_A$ | Controlled logarithmic Lipschitz oscillation at the heat scale; independent of base point and datum lower bound. |
| q-positive-curvature-radius | Radius multiplied by the square root of positive curvature | < | $\pi/2$ | Positive sectional lower bound; the radius is independent of the center. |
| q-radial-gradient-norm | Gradient norm of half squared distance | = | $r$ | Almost everywhere, including the zero-speed case. |
| q-geodesic-model-operator-bound | Modified-distance differential operator along a geodesic | ≤ | $\ell^2$ | Constant-speed minimizing geodesics contained in the modification ball. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-test-approximation** [claim](#stmt-pre-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-approximation): Good cutoffs, heat regularization, spectral continuity, and the integrated Bochner estimate. -> Compact generator core and local scalar and weight approximations.
- **step-tensor-continuity** [claim](#stmt-pre-tensor-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-tensor-continuity): Expand the weighted covariant derivative and pass products using strong convergence and domination. -> Continuity of the integrated curvature evaluation.
- **step-short-flows** [claim](#stmt-fwd-flow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-flow): Regular Lagrangian flow theory and repeated use of the continuity equation. -> Density variations and endpoint expansions.
- **step-configuration-comparison** [claim](#stmt-fwd-configuration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-configuration): Alexandrov angle comparison, model cosine expansion, and norming Lipschitz functions. -> Geometric lower bound for the averaged energy difference.
- **step-optimal-density-variation** [claim](#stmt-fwd-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-density): Restrict optimal plans to recover pointwise distortion, then use semiconcavity and the continuity equation. -> Density first variation and strong rescaled velocity convergence.
- **step-dual-energy-expansion** [claim](#stmt-fwd-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-dual): Apply the exact energy identity to fixed Sobolev dual potentials and retain the radial defect. -> Pair lower expansion and radial equality with square defect.
- **step-square-and-curvature** [claim](#stmt-fwd-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-algebra): Sobolev integration by parts, separate polarization, and centered averaging. -> Curvature contraction plus nonnegative localization square.
- **step-defective-curvature** [claim](#stmt-fwd-defect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-defect): Cancel lower-order terms, take the short-flow limit, then approximate endpoint acceleration corrections. -> Localized defective curvature inequality.
- **step-semiconcave-weights** [claim](#stmt-fwd-weight-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-weight-approximation): Inf-convolution with squared distance and weak Sobolev compactness. -> Extension of the defect inequality to Lipschitz weights.
- **step-gradient-localization** [claim](#stmt-fwd-gradient-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-gradient-selection): Approximate tangent-module fields by piecewise gradients and replace a measurable partition by Lipschitz weights. -> Finite gradient choices with arbitrarily small weighted error.
- **step-compact-gradient-bound** [claim](#stmt-fwd-gradient-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-gradient-bound): Sum localized defective inequalities and let the gradient selection error vanish. -> Sectional lower bound on compact test gradients.
- **step-variable-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-fwd-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-coefficients): Positive Radon representation, polynomial interpolation, and rational-pair positivity. -> Curvature bound for variable test coefficients.
- **step-field-approximation** [claim](#stmt-fwd-field-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-field-approximation): Approximate finitely many potentials while keeping test coefficients fixed and use product rules. -> Local strong convergence of fields, derivatives, and divergences.
- **step-global-forward-bound** [claim](#stmt-fwd-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fwd-field-approximation-2): Pass local field approximations through tensor continuity, then exhaust the global weight. -> Forward implication on the original global test classes.
- **step-heat-inputs** [claim](#stmt-heat-analytic-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-analytic-inputs): Semigroup splitting and local cutoff approximation of bounded data. -> Heat regularization and local logarithmic estimates.
- **step-heat-factor-equations** [claim](#stmt-heat-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-regularity): Apply generator regularization, logarithmic chain rules, and the heat semigroup identities. -> Regularity, continuity equation, uniform gradients, and terminal traces.
- **step-terminal-kernel-ratio** [claim](#stmt-heat-kernel-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-kernel-ratio): Combine Gaussian kernel bounds, annular volume comparison, and logarithmic Lipschitz control. -> Uniform heat moment ratio.
- **step-density-laplacian-control** [claim](#stmt-heat-density-laplacian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-density-laplacian): Integrate the density estimate backward from the fixed terminal datum and split the terminal layer for the Laplacian bound. -> Bounded compression and integrable vanishing lower error.
- **step-weighted-second-order-bounds** [claim](#stmt-heat-weighted-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-weighted-estimates): Integrated Bochner identity, absorption of cutoff errors, terminal traces, and a divergence estimate. -> Terminal-uniform weighted second-order bounds and vanishing density-gradient products.
- **step-weak-riccati** [claim](#stmt-heat-riccati-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-riccati-identity): Identify the curvature terms through first-order products and differentiate the weighted Hessian in the local graph norm. -> Weak Riccati evolution.
- **step-acceleration-error-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-heat-error-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-error-cancellation): Cancel inverse-viscosity terms and separate a nonnegative Bochner measure from a Sobolev remainder. -> Vanishing lower error uniformly in terminal exhaustion.
- **step-viscous-index** [claim](#stmt-heat-viscous-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-viscous-index): Complete the Riccati square, apply the tensor lower bound, and take the fixed-viscosity terminal trace. -> Viscous index inequality.
- **step-logarithmic-heat-limit** [claim](#stmt-heat-laplace-principle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-laplace-principle): Gaussian Laplace bounds followed by local Sobolev compactness. -> Hopf–Lax limit of logarithmic heat potentials.
- **step-density-curve-compactness** [claim](#stmt-heat-curve-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-curve-compactness): Radial domination yields moment tails and Wasserstein continuity before applying the continuity-equation criterion and compactness. -> Subsequential density curves with positive-time compression.
- **step-action-and-velocity-limit** [claim](#stmt-heat-action-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-action-limit): Weak flux compactness and an exact action sandwich force equality of kinetic norms. -> Geodesic limits and strong weighted velocity convergence.
- **step-common-calibrated-paths** [claim](#stmt-heat-common-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-common-paths): Optimal dynamical transport uniqueness and equality of path and conditional velocity actions. -> Common Borel path prescription and unaveraged path velocity.
- **step-zero-viscosity-index** [claim](#stmt-heat-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heat-common-paths-2): Pass fixed trial energies by strong coefficient-weighted velocity convergence, then remove the positive terminal perturbation. -> Index inequality for compact nonnegative Lipschitz terminal weights.
- **step-local-content-nullity** [claim](#stmt-frames-content-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-content-null): Iterate content covers with shrinking total cost and use separability. -> Hausdorff nullity without assuming finite Hausdorff measure.
- **step-rank-defect-energy-content** [claim](#stmt-frames-energy-content) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-energy-content): Telescope Sobolev averages and select disjoint energy-detecting balls. -> Energy bound on rank-defect Hausdorff content.
- **step-countable-frames** [claim](#stmt-frames-countable-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-energy-content-2): Select harmonic splitting tuples at every dyadic scale, choose common precise representatives, and control rank-defect content. -> Countable frames outside polar and Hausdorff-null exceptional sets.
- **step-entire-path-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-frames-path-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-path-avoidance): Bound pathwise Sobolev suprema and tube incidence, then pass rapid Lipschitz approximations. -> Entire-path avoidance and common continuous Sobolev representatives.
- **step-every-time-regularity** [claim](#stmt-frames-regular-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-regular-paths): Use endpoint and dense-time regularity, then tangent-cone continuity along geodesics. -> Regularity at every time on almost every path.
- **step-path-fibers-and-connection** [claim](#stmt-frames-path-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-path-calculus): Build fibers from Gram forms, solve connection ODEs, and obtain velocity and endpoint traces from scalar second differentiation. -> Parallel orthonormal frames and continuous parallel velocity.
- **step-countable-trial-density** [claim](#stmt-frames-trial-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-trial-density): Subtract an endpoint-preserving trial and approximate zero-trace coefficients on rational frame intervals. -> Countable global trials dense separately along almost every path.
- **step-radial-terminal-trace** [claim](#stmt-frames-terminal-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-terminal-trace): Approximate squared distance by test functions and pass endpoint difference quotients in the prescribed order. -> Terminal velocity equals the squared-distance gradient.
- **step-radial-hessian-comparison** [claim](#stmt-frames-hessian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frames-hessian): Extract a signed Radon measure, take a countable trial-energy infimum, and insert the pathwise Jacobi trial. -> Radial Hessian bound with nonpositive singular part.
- **step-every-geodesic-interface** [claim](#stmt-weight-every-geodesic) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Invoke the companion weak-Hessian theorem with its bounded global potential and continuous bound hypotheses. -> Distributional upper second-derivative bound on every minimizing geodesic.
- **step-bounded-distance-modification** [claim](#stmt-weight-distance-modification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weight-distance-modification): Use a monotone smooth cutoff in squared distance and the weak Hessian chain rule. -> Bounded global potential and continuous Hessian bound preserving the local model distance.
- **step-alexandrov-globalization** [claim](#stmt-weight-alexandrov) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weight-alexandrov): Compare the model differential equation, derive local four-point comparison, and apply Alexandrov globalization. -> Alexandrov lower curvature bound and Hausdorff dimension.
- **step-main-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-main-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weight-alexandrov-2): Combine the forward global tensor bound with the converse index, Hessian, and triangle-comparison chain. -> The stated characterization equivalence.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-global-characterization**: Alexandrov lower curvature comparison is equivalent to the distributional sectional lower bound in the original global test classes, with full support, exact Hausdorff measure, and the stated unreduced curvature-dimension condition.
- **invariant-common-calibrated-paths**: The calibrated path prescription is independent of the trial and terminal density. Compact terminal data retain bounded speed and compression on positive-time intervals.
- **invariant-pathwise-trial-density**: A countable collection of global trials with prescribed endpoint identities approximates admissible Sobolev fields separately along almost every qualifying path. The approximating sequence may depend on the path.
- **invariant-hessian-measure-comparison**: The distributional Hessian of half-squared distance, evaluated on a compact test gradient, is a signed Radon measure with nonpositive singular part and the stated radial and transverse comparison bound.
- **invariant-every-geodesic-transfer**: A global weak Hessian upper bound for a bounded globally Lipschitz function, with a bounded continuous comparison function, implies the distributional second-derivative bound along every constant-speed minimizing geodesic.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-equivalent-reference-measure**: Apply the characterization after replacing Hausdorff measure by an arbitrary equivalent weighted measure. -> The exact measure hypothesis supplying the noncollapsed structure has not been established.
- **anti-use-finite-test-certificate**: Certify the global sectional lower bound from a finite collection of sampled fields and weights. -> Finite sampling does not establish the universal global test-class hypothesis.
- **anti-use-interchanged-forward-limits**: Merge the short-flow, correction, localization, and exhaustion limits into a uniform computational approximation. -> The proof does not provide uniform divergence or partition-gradient bounds across these approximation stages.
- **anti-use-weak-velocity-energy-limit**: Pass the quadratic index energy to the limit using weak velocity convergence alone. -> Weak convergence does not supply the convergence of quadratic velocity terms required by the index argument.
- **anti-use-integrated-trial-density**: Use the countable global trial collection as an asserted simultaneous approximation of fields in an integrated space along the plan. -> The available approximation is pathwise, and its sequence may vary with the path.
- **anti-use-dirac-endpoint-compression**: Extend the bounded-compression path calculus to the radial Dirac endpoint without restricting the time interval. -> The positive-time compression estimate does not establish bounded compression at the singular endpoint.
- **anti-use-unbounded-distance-transfer**: Apply the every-geodesic transfer directly to an unbounded distance potential. -> The transfer theorem requires a bounded globally Lipschitz potential and a bounded continuous comparison function.
- **anti-use-additive-bochner-squares**: Combine the Hessian and dimension Bochner estimates by adding both squared terms with their original coefficients. -> The source supplies separate inequalities or their convex combinations, not the stronger additive inequality.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The characterization and its analytic bridge quantify over global Sobolev test classes and minimizing geodesics on metric measure spaces. The source states: "The construction uses successive limits, not a single uniform expansion over its approximation parameters." It also states: "No simultaneous approximation in an integrated space of fields along the plan is asserted." The finite averaging in the forward proof does not provide a finite representation of the ambient space or a terminating decision procedure for the quantified curvature hypothesis. An executable certification construction for the characterization is ABSENT.
